What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I came in because for a while now, I’ve been convinced that certain people in the arts and finance circles here in Cincinnati are quietly tracking which clients I meet with. It seems like they're sending me little signals through event invitations, panel topics, or even just who sits near me at networking events. \n\nI try to rationalize it, and even when I tell myself it could just be a coincidence, I just can’t really let it go. It's been weighing on me, so I figured I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these people are actually tracking you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, I am genuinely convinced it's actually happening. I mean, I try to step back and tell myself it could just be a coincidence. I really do. But... the things they do, like the specific panel topics or the way they seat people at these events, it just lines up too perfectly with my client list. \n\nSo, yes, I believe they are actually doing it. Even when I try to brush it off as just a feeling or my imagination, I just can't let it go. It feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific goal or purpose behind why these people are tracking you?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. That’s... that's the part I can't really figure out. I've thought about it a lot, trying to see what the angle is, or why they would care so much about my specific clients. But I don't have a clear answer. \n\nI don't know what the end goal is, or what they're trying to get out of it. I just know what I'm seeing at these events, but the actual reason behind it... I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I don't hear voices or any sounds that aren't there. It's really just the things I see happening around me at these events.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be true, even if others around you disagree or think they're unlikely?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's really just... just this one issue with the events and my clients. I don't have any other theories or beliefs that people would find strange, or that I disagree with anyone about. Everything else in my day-to-day life is pretty standard. It's just this one specific situation that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that you might be overestimating the importance of these events or the interest people have in your client list?\n\nPatient: I mean, I try to. I really do. Sometimes I tell myself it's just a coincidence, you know? That maybe I'm reading too much into it. But... I just can't really let it go. \n\nIt's been going on for a while now. I'm just convinced that these people—specifically in the arts and finance circles here in Cincinnati—are quietly tracking who I meet with. And they send me these little signals. It's in the invitations I get, the topics they choose for panels, even who happens to sit near me at these events. So, even when I try to tell myself I'm overestimating it, I just keep seeing it happen.\n\nDoctor: Is this belief affecting your ability to work or interact with people in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I'm still working, but... yeah, it has affected things. My practice has definitely gotten smaller. I just find myself missing follow-ups with clients because I'm distracted by all of this. And I've started avoiding some of the public panels and events altogether, just so I don't have to deal with the signals. It's just... I have a lot of trouble keeping up the same level of client contact that I used to manage easily. So, yeah, it's taking a toll on my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things you see or hear in public, such as ads, news stories, or songs, are somehow related to you or your situation?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. Ads and the news seem normal to me. It's really just the specific people at those events in Cincinnati, like I was saying. I don't feel like the TV or the radio is talking to me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people tracking you are working together in an organized way?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they're... like, having meetings about it or anything. I haven't seen any proof of a big organized group. It's more just... I notice the individuals doing it. Like they all just happen to be doing the same thing, sending those same little signals. But I don't know if they're actually working together on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you doubt that these people are actually tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Sometimes I try to tell myself that it could all just be a coincidence. Like, maybe those people in the arts and finance circles here aren't actually tracking which clients I meet with. Maybe the specific invitations I get, or the panel topics, or even who happens to sit near me at these events... maybe it's all just random. \n\nBut... even when I tell myself that, I can't really let it go. I'm still convinced they're quietly sending me those little signals. It's actually why I came in to see you in the first place, because this has been going on for a while now and no matter how much I try to rationalize it or doubt it, I just can't shake the feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that people are talking about you or your situation when you're not there?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about that. I mean, I guess it's possible they talk about me when I'm not around, but I don't have any reason to think they are. I've never overheard anything or had anyone tell me they were talking about me behind my back. It's really just those little signals they give off when I'm actually there at the events. I don't know what they do on their own time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It seems like they're sending me little signals through event invitations, panel topics, or even just who sits near me at networking events. \n\nI try to rationalize it, and even when I tell myself it could just be a coincidence, I just can’t really let it go. It's been weighing on me, so I figured I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these people are actually tracking you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, I am genuinely convinced it's actually happening. I mean, I try to step back and tell myself it could just be a coincidence. I really do. But... the things they do, like the specific panel topics or the way they seat people at these events, it just lines up too perfectly with my client list. \n\nSo, yes, I believe they are actually doing it. Even when I try to brush it off as just a feeling or my imagination, I just can't let it go. It feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific goal or purpose behind why these people are tracking you?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. That’s... that's the part I can't really figure out. I've thought about it a lot, trying to see what the angle is, or why they would care so much about my specific clients. But I don't have a clear answer. \n\nI don't know what the end goal is, or what they're trying to get out of it. I just know what I'm seeing at these events, but the actual reason behind it... I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I don't hear voices or any sounds that aren't there. It's really just the things I see happening around me at these events.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be true, even if others around you disagree or think they're unlikely?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's really just... just this one issue with the events and my clients. I don't have any other theories or beliefs that people would find strange, or that I disagree with anyone about. Everything else in my day-to-day life is pretty standard. It's just this one specific situation that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that you might be overestimating the importance of these events or the interest people have in your client list?\n\nPatient: I mean, I try to. I really do. Sometimes I tell myself it's just a coincidence, you know? That maybe I'm reading too much into it. But... I just can't really let it go. \n\nIt's been going on for a while now. I'm just convinced that these people—specifically in the arts and finance circles here in Cincinnati—are quietly tracking who I meet with. And they send me these little signals. It's in the invitations I get, the topics they choose for panels, even who happens to sit near me at these events. I have a lot of trouble keeping up the same level of client contact that I used to manage easily. So, yeah, it's taking a toll on my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things you see or hear in public, such as ads, news stories, or songs, are somehow related to you or your situation?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. Ads and the news seem normal to me. It's really just the specific people at those events in Cincinnati, like I was saying. I don't feel like the TV or the radio is talking to me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people tracking you are working together in an organized way?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they're... like, having meetings about it or anything. Sometimes I try to tell myself that it could all just be a coincidence. Like, maybe those people in the arts and finance circles here aren't actually tracking which clients I meet with. Maybe the specific invitations I get, or the panel topics, or even who happens to sit near me at these events... maybe it's all just random. \n\nBut... even when I tell myself that, I can't really let it go. I'm still convinced they're quietly sending me those little signals. I mean, I guess it's possible they talk about me when I'm not around, but I don't have any reason to think they are. I've never overheard anything or had anyone tell me they were talking about me behind my back. It's really just those little signals they give off when I'm actually there at the events. It feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific goal or purpose behind why these people are tracking you?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. That’s... that's the part I can't really figure out. I've thought about it a lot, trying to see what the angle is, or why they would care so much about my specific clients. But I don't have a clear answer. \n\nI don't know what the end goal is, or what they're trying to get out of it. I just know what I'm seeing at these events, but the actual reason behind it... I'm really not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I don't hear voices or any sounds that aren't there. It's really just the things I see happening around me at these events.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be true, even if others around you disagree or think they're unlikely?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's really just... just this one issue with the events and my clients. I don't have any other theories or beliefs that people would find strange, or that I disagree with anyone about. Everything else in my day-to-day life is pretty standard. It's just this one specific situation that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered the possibility that you might be overestimating the importance of these events or the interest people have in your client list?\n\nPatient: I mean, I try to. I really do. Sometimes I tell myself it's just a coincidence, you know? That maybe I'm reading too much into it. But... I just can't really let it go. \n\nIt's been going on for a while now. I'm just convinced that these people—specifically in the arts and finance circles here in Cincinnati—are quietly tracking who I meet with. And they send me these little signals. It's in the invitations I get, the topics they choose for panels, even who happens to sit near me at these events. So, even when I try to tell myself I'm overestimating it, I just keep seeing it happen.\n\nDoctor: Is this belief affecting your ability to work or interact with people in your daily life?\n\nPatient: I'm still working, but... yeah, it has affected things. My practice has definitely gotten smaller. I just find myself missing follow-ups with clients because I'm distracted by all of this. And I've started avoiding some of the public panels and events altogether, just so I don't have to deal with the signals. It's just... I have a lot of trouble keeping up the same level of client contact that I used to manage easily. So, yeah, it's taking a toll on my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things you see or hear in public, such as ads, news stories, or songs, are somehow related to you or your situation?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. Ads and the news seem normal to me. It's really just the specific people at those events in Cincinnati, like I was saying. I don't feel like the TV or the radio is talking to me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people tracking you are working together in an organized way?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if they're... like, having meetings about it or anything. I haven't seen any proof of a big organized group. It's more just... I notice the individuals doing it. Like they all just happen to be doing the same thing, sending those same little signals. But I don't know if they're actually working together on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have moments where you doubt that these people are actually tracking you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Sometimes I try to tell myself that it could all just be a coincidence. Like, maybe those people in the arts and finance circles here aren't actually tracking which clients I meet with. Maybe the specific invitations I get, or the panel topics, or even who happens to sit near me at these events... maybe it's all just random. \n\nBut... even when I tell myself that, I can't really let it go. I'm still convinced they're quietly sending me those little signals. It's actually why I came in to see you in the first place, because this has been going on for a while now and no matter how much I try to rationalize it or doubt it, I just can't shake the feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that people are talking about you or your situation when you're not there?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about that. I mean, I guess it's possible they talk about me when I'm not around, but I don't have any reason to think they are. I've never overheard anything or had anyone tell me they were talking about me behind my back. It's really just those little signals they give off when I'm actually there at the events. I don't know what they do on their own time.\n\nDoctor: Has this belief about being tracked affected your personal relationships with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven't really talked to my friends or family about it. I try to keep my work and those networking events separate from my personal life anyway. So... no, things with them are pretty much the same as always. It hasn't caused any problems at home or with my friends or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble with daily activities outside of work due to these concerns?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... outside of work, things have just gotten a bit harder to keep up with. I mean, I still get my work done, mostly, but I have to do it in pieces. I just don't have that same spark I used to. Like, I'll leave client follow-ups sitting on my desk longer than I should. \n\nAnd outside of the office... I just don't really do much anymore. I used to go to jazz nights, or play pickup basketball, but lately I just skip them. And honestly, I don't even feel bad about missing them. I just don't care. Even when my friends try to check in and ask what's going on, I just give them really short answers. It's not that I'm mad at them or anything, it just... talking feels like it takes way more effort than it should right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
